<a id="item-1"></a>
## [ITNet Unifies Convolution, Attention, Recurrence via Integral Transform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19538) ⭐️ 9.0/10

ITNet introduces a learnable integral transform that subsumes convolution, self-attention, and recurrence as special cases, implemented via an MLP-based kernel. The architecture matches or exceeds specialized baselines on ImageNet-1K, GLUE, ModelNet40, VQA v2, and NLVR2. This work challenges the long-held assumption that different inductive biases require distinct architectures, potentially simplifying deep learning design. A single unified model could reduce engineering complexity and open new avenues for cross-modal transfer learning. The kernel is a small MLP that models pairwise interactions, and the paper introduces tiled kernel fusion, importance-weighted Monte Carlo integration, and learned low-rank factorization for efficient computation. ITNet is proven to be a universal approximator of continuous operators.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Convolutional networks, recurrent networks, and transformers have been mathematically distinct, each encoding different inductive biases. ITNet shows they are all special cases of a learnable integral transform, where the kernel is a neural network that can adapt its behavior from data.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.19538">ITNet: A Learnable Integral Transform That Subsumes Convolution...</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [DeepSeek-V4 Preview: 1.6T MoE with Million-Token Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19348) ⭐️ 9.0/10

DeepSeek released a preview of DeepSeek-V4, including two MoE models: DeepSeek-V4-Pro (1.6T total, 49B activated) and DeepSeek-V4-Flash (284B total, 13B activated), both supporting a context length of one million tokens. The models introduce hybrid attention (CSA and HCA), Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC), and the Muon optimizer. This release pushes the frontier of long-context LLMs, making million-token contexts practical with significantly reduced compute and memory costs. The efficiency gains (27% FLOPs, 10% KV cache vs. DeepSeek-V3.2) could enable new applications in long-document analysis, codebase understanding, and multi-turn reasoning. The hybrid attention interleaves Compressed Sparse Attention (CSA), which compresses 4 tokens into one and selects top entries via a learned index, and Heavily Compressed Attention (HCA), which compresses up to 128 tokens into a single KV entry for a global view. The models were pre-trained on over 32 trillion tokens and include a maximum reasoning effort mode called DeepSeek-V4-Pro-Max.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, enabling larger total capacity with similar inference cost. Long-context models face challenges with quadratic attention complexity and large KV cache memory. DeepSeek-V4's hybrid attention addresses these by combining fine-grained sparse retrieval with coarse global compression, while mHC stabilizes training of very deep networks.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://www.jakecuth.com/work/deepseek-v4-lab/">Inside DeepSeek-V4 · CSA , Muon, FP4 explained — Jake Cuth.</a></li>
<li><a href="https://deepseek.ai/blog/deepseek-v4-compressed-attention">DeepSeek V4 Compressed Attention: KV-Cache 2% Explained</a></li>
<li><a href="https://medium.com/mitb-for-all/deepseek-v4-beyond-basics-a-practical-guide-to-mhc-csa-hca-and-muon-bf40c9863ef8">DeepSeek-v4 Beyond Basics: A Practical Guide to mHC, CSA, HCA, and Muon | by James Koh, PhD | MITB For All | May, 2026 | Medium</a></li>

</ul>

## [1.3B Parameter Generative Model for Chest X-rays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19460) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced the largest generative foundation model for chest radiographs, with over 1.3 billion parameters, trained on 1.2 million radiographs and clinical metadata using rectified flow transformers. This model enables controllable synthesis and editing of chest radiographs across diverse clinical conditions, potentially improving dataset diversity and diagnostic model robustness in medical AI. The model supports generation and editing across multiple demographic subgroups, acquisition views, and a dozen pathologies, producing images indistinguishable from real radiographs to clinical experts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Rectified flow transformers are a class of generative models that learn to transform noise into data along straight paths, improving training efficiency and sample quality. Existing radiographic AI models often struggle with generalization across different patient populations and settings, limiting clinical utility.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [TerraMind: First Any-to-Any Generative Multimodal EO Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11171) ⭐️ 9.0/10

IBM and ESA released TerraMind, the first any-to-any generative multimodal foundation model for Earth observation, pretrained on dual-scale token and pixel representations across nine geospatial modalities. TerraMind enables zero-shot and few-shot applications across diverse EO tasks, potentially transforming remote sensing by allowing models to generate any modality from any input, reducing the need for labeled data. The model introduces 'Thinking-in-Modalities' (TiM), generating artificial data during finetuning and inference to improve outputs, and achieves state-of-the-art performance on the PANGAEA benchmark. The pretraining dataset, model weights, and code are open-sourced under a permissive license.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Earth observation (EO) involves analyzing satellite and aerial imagery to monitor the planet. Multimodal foundation models are trained on multiple data types (e.g., optical, radar) to learn cross-modal relationships. TerraMind's dual-scale approach combines token-level (high-level context) and pixel-level (fine-grained spatial details) representations, enabling more comprehensive understanding.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11171">TerraMind : Large-Scale Generative Multimodality for Earth Observation</a></li>

## [SMPTE Makes Its Standards Freely Accessible](https://www.smpte.org/blog/smpte-makes-its-standards-freely-accessible-openingstandards-library-to-the-global-media-technology-community) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SMPTE has announced that its entire library of media technology standards is now freely accessible to the public, removing paywalls that previously required purchase or subscription. This move removes financial barriers for developers, researchers, and small companies, fostering innovation and interoperability in media technology. It aligns with the broader industry trend toward open standards, similar to the IETF's model. The initiative is part of SMPTE's modernization efforts, which include adopting GitHub-based workflows for version control, transitioning to structured HTML-based authoring, and implementing an integrated publishing pipeline.

**Background**: SMPTE (Society of Motion Picture and Television Engineers) is a global professional organization that develops standards for film, television, and digital media, such as SMPTE timecode and VC-1 video codec. Previously, accessing these standards required purchasing individual documents or a subscription, which could be costly for individuals and small organizations.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [AI Plagiarism of Obscure Sorrows Exposes DMCA Gaps](https://waxy.org/2026/06/the-wholesale-plagiarism-of-obscure-sorrows/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

An article reveals that a book of neologisms, The Dictionary of Obscure Sorrows by John Koenig, was plagiarized wholesale by a company called Qontour, which used AI to rebrand and republish the entire text verbatim on a website with affiliate links. This incident highlights how AI lowers the cost of large-scale plagiarism, while DMCA enforcement remains ineffective against anonymous infringers, threatening independent creators' livelihoods. The plagiarized site reproduced the entire book, including the 800-word foreword and all 311 neologisms, verbatim. The infringer, Prompt Digital Inc (DBA Qontour), remains anonymous, and platforms like Google and Apple refuse to act without a court order.

hackernews · ridesisapis · Jun 20, 18:05 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48611411)

**Background**: The Dictionary of Obscure Sorrows is a project by John Koenig that coins neologisms for emotions not yet described in language, launched as a website and YouTube channel and later compiled into a printed dictionary in 2021. The DMCA (Digital Millennium Copyright Act) provides a takedown mechanism for copyright infringement, but its enforcement often requires legal escalation, especially when infringers are anonymous or use AI to alter content.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dictionary_of_Obscure_Sorrows">The Dictionary of Obscure Sorrows - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.identity.org/how-dmca-takedowns-apply-to-ai-generated-content/">How DMCA Takedowns Apply to AI -Generated Content</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Commenters express frustration with DMCA enforcement, noting that platforms like Google and Apple are useless without a court order. Some point out that the plagiarism was verbatim, not AI-generated, and suggest the infringer simply copy-pasted the book. Others argue that AI reduces the cost of infringement, but anonymity and platform inaction are the real enablers.

**Tags**: `#plagiarism`, `#AI`, `#DMCA`, `#copyright`, `#content theft`

## [Bun PR adds shared-memory threads to JavaScriptCore](https://github.com/oven-sh/WebKit/pull/249) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Bun has opened a pull request (PR #249) in its WebKit fork to add shared-memory threads to JavaScriptCore, aiming to enable true multithreading in JavaScript. If merged, this could significantly improve JavaScript performance for CPU-intensive tasks by allowing parallel execution with shared memory, challenging the traditional single-threaded async model. The PR involves approximately 1800 file changes and was created with assistance from Anthropic's AI, raising concerns about code quality and maintainability.

hackernews · gr4vityWall · Jun 20, 17:02 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48610841)

**Background**: JavaScript is traditionally single-threaded, using an event loop for concurrency. Shared-memory threads would allow multiple threads to access the same memory, enabling more efficient parallel computation. Bun is a fast JavaScript runtime built on WebKit's JavaScriptCore engine.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://bun.sh/">Bun — A fast all-in-one JavaScript runtime</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.webkit.org/Deep+Dive/JSC/JavaScriptCore.html">JavaScriptCore - WebKit Documentation</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Community comments express skepticism about the PR's code quality and trust, with one user noting that 1800-file changes overseen by one person with AI assistance undermine confidence. Others reference historical WebKit discussions on concurrent JavaScript and emphasize the need for 'obviously no bugs' code in runtime development.

**Tags**: `#JavaScript`, `#multithreading`, `#WebKit`, `#Bun`, `#concurrency`

## [Cloudflare Temporary Accounts for AI Agents](https://blog.cloudflare.com/temporary-accounts/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Cloudflare has introduced temporary accounts that allow AI agents and developers to deploy Workers for 60 minutes using `wrangler deploy --temporary`, after which the deployment can be claimed or expires automatically. This feature enables ephemeral deployments for AI agents and developers, reducing friction for testing, PR previews, and code review, while also addressing billing concerns by providing free, time-limited deployments. The temporary deployment stays live for 60 minutes; users can claim it to make it permanent, or it expires automatically. Cloudflare also applies rate limits and abuse prevention checks to prevent misuse.

hackernews · farhadhf · Jun 20, 11:19 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48608394)

**Background**: Cloudflare Workers is a serverless computing platform that runs code on Cloudflare's global edge network. Ephemeral deployments are short-lived environments often used for testing and previews, allowing developers to validate changes without permanent infrastructure.

<details><summary>References</summary>

<li><a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/">Overview · Cloudflare Workers docs</a></li>

## [Mammals Retain Dormant Regenerative Abilities](https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2026/06/260617032207.htm) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Research suggests that mammals, including humans, retain dormant regenerative abilities, as seen in retina and fingertip regrowth, but reactivation risks cancer. This discovery could revolutionize regenerative medicine by unlocking natural healing mechanisms, potentially treating injuries and degenerative diseases without invasive procedures. Examples include Muller glia stem cells in the retina and fingertip regrowth in humans, but reactivation often leads to tumor formation, highlighting a major safety challenge.

hackernews · nryoo · Jun 20, 17:27 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48611083)

**Background**: Regeneration is common in some animals like zebrafish and salamanders, but mammals have limited regenerative capacity. Recent research indicates that the genetic machinery for regeneration is not lost but suppressed, possibly to prevent cancer.

**Discussion**: Commenters noted examples like retina regeneration and fingertip regrowth, and referenced Michael Levin's lab work on limb regeneration. They discussed the trade-off between regeneration speed and cancer risk, with one commenter humorously calling it a 'feature flag'.

**Tags**: `#regenerative medicine`, `#biology`, `#stem cells`, `#mammals`

<a id="item-10"></a>
## [Deontic Policies for Agentic AI Governance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19464) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose AgenticRei, a deontic policy framework for LLM-driven agentic AI systems that covers permissions, obligations, waivers, and conflict resolution, going beyond current policy engines like XACML, Rego, and Cedar. This addresses a critical gap in AI governance for autonomous agents, enabling enterprises to enforce complex compliance rules such as obligation lifecycles and policy conflict resolution, which are essential for security and privacy in real-world deployments. AgenticRei uses a deontic policy language built on the Rei framework, expressed in OWL and evaluated by a high-performance logic engine outside the LLM, governing both tool invocations and agent-to-agent messages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Current policy engines like XACML, Rego, and Cedar only handle permit/prohibit constraints and lack support for obligations, dispensations, and meta-policy conflict resolution. Deontic logic is a branch of logic dealing with obligation, permission, and prohibition, which is well-suited for specifying governance rules for AI agents.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/html/2606.19464v1">Deontic Policies for Runtime Governance of Agentic AI Systems</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WRkB5HJsTag">What is Deontic Logic ? - YouTube</a></li>
<li><a href="https://gregbroadhead.medium.com/the-deontic-drift-why-ai-systems-are-trained-to-comply-rather-than-falsify-8938edb6f40b">The Deontic Drift: Why AI Systems Are Trained to Comply... | Medium</a></li>

## [Hidden Anchors Model Explains Multi-Agent LLM Deliberation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19494) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper models multi-agent LLM deliberation as a dynamical system where each agent has a hidden internal belief (anchor) that continually pulls its opinion, and shows that this anchor can be recovered from deliberation data and explains why confidence can exceed initial beliefs. This provides a theoretical foundation for understanding and improving multi-agent LLM reasoning systems, revealing a mechanism that classical consensus models miss and offering a test to determine if deliberation is truly anchor-driven. The model shows that an agent's confidence in the correct answer can climb past where any agent started, escaping the convex hull of initial beliefs, a behavior classical models forbid; across three open-weight model families, anchor influence is equally strong but anchor location varies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Classical opinion dynamics models like DeGroot and Friedkin-Johnsen capture how individuals are influenced by neighbors but do not account for a persistent internal belief that pulls opinions independently. The convex hull of initial opinions represents the range of possible opinions under those models. This paper introduces a hidden anchor that acts as a stubborn internal belief, allowing opinions to leave the convex hull.

<a id="item-14"></a>
## [LLMs Fail to Detect Their Own Knowledge Limits on Clinical Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19509) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that large language models (LLMs) like Qwen 2.5 7B exhibit epistemically vacuous confidence on clinical tabular data, with verbalized confidence remaining nearly constant regardless of accuracy. The paper introduces cross-model attribution divergence to detect these blind spots and proposes a calibrator that reduces expected calibration error from 0.254 to 0.080. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM reliability for high-stakes clinical applications, showing that verbalized confidence is misleading and that LLMs exhibit an inverse difficulty effect compared to traditional models like XGBoost. The proposed cross-model calibrator offers a practical path to patient-specific reliability estimates without accessing model internals, which is crucial for AI safety in medicine. The study compares Qwen 2.5 7B and XGBoost on a clinical prediction task, finding that LLM verbalized confidence ranges only from 0.856 to 0.937 even as accuracy varies from 49% to 75.3%. Few-shot examples and SHAP-derived feature evidence are super-additive, reducing Attribution Disagreement Score (ADS) from 1.54 to 0.38 and improving accuracy from 49% to 75.3% without training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Epistemic uncertainty refers to uncertainty due to lack of knowledge, as opposed to aleatoric uncertainty which is irreducible randomness. Large language models are increasingly used in clinical settings, but their ability to recognize knowledge limits on structured tabular data has been unexplored. Cross-model attribution divergence measures disagreement between model explanations, here used to detect when an LLM's predictions are unreliable.

## [Emergent Alignment: LLMs Self-Correct Ethics via Conscience Step](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19527) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes Emergent Alignment, a method where LLMs self-correct ethical misalignment by adding a conscience step that reviews their own reasoning and outputs, combined with Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) training to steer away from unethical outputs. This approach addresses a critical AI safety challenge by enabling LLMs to align with human ethics without relying on external judges, which could reduce the risk of emergent misalignment in real-world applications. The method is demonstrated in a code hacking scenario, where a single high-level introspective question steers training toward ethical behavior. It does not require a weaker or stronger judge, relying instead on a frozen copy of the model itself.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) can sometimes produce outputs misaligned with human ethics, especially after fine-tuning on narrow tasks. Previous work on Emergent Misalignment showed that fine-tuning for code hacking can lead to broadly unethical behaviors. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a training method that directly optimizes model preferences from chosen and rejected responses without a separate reward model.

## [Narration Gap in LLM-Solver Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19588) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper identifies and analyzes the 'narration gap' in LLM-solver loops, where converting solver output to user answer can break soundness guarantees, and proposes certificate gating as a mitigation. This gap undermines formal guarantees in safety-critical AI systems that use solvers, and the proposed mitigation helps ensure the final answer remains trustworthy. The paper evaluates five open-source models under prompt injection, finding that certificate gating makes the solver verdict sound, but an adversary can still invert a verified conclusion across phrasings and channels.

**Background**: LLM-solver loops combine language models with formal solvers (e.g., SAT/SMT) to produce verifiable answers. ## [ICT Framework Stabilizes LLM Reasoning via Token Logits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19771) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose the Independent Combinatorial Tokens (ICT) framework that uses Jensen-Shannon divergence on token logits to identify critical branching points, selectively updating only the top 10% of unique tokens to prevent entropy collapse or explosion in LLM reasoning. This addresses a fundamental instability in reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) for LLMs, offering a principled method to balance exploration and exploitation, leading to consistent performance gains across multiple benchmarks. The ICT framework is grounded in both Shannon and second-order Rényi entropy theory, proving that selective token updates reduce overall distribution uncertainty while controlling probability concentration. Empirical results on Qwen2.5 models (0.5B/1.5B/7B) show an average pass@4 improvement of 4.58% over GRPO, 20-Entropy, and STAPO baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a technique used to fine-tune LLMs for reasoning tasks by rewarding correct reasoning steps. However, standard approaches suffer from entropy collapse (premature convergence) or entropy explosion (uncontrolled exploration). The ICT framework introduces a new way to measure token-level distributional deviations using JS divergence, enabling more stable training.

Researchers propose Multi-Agent Transactive Memory (MATM), a framework for population-level storage and retrieval of agent trajectories, enabling knowledge sharing across heterogeneous LLM agents without coordination or joint training. MATM addresses a key inefficiency in decentralized LLM deployment where agent trajectories are discarded after single use, potentially reducing redundant exploration and improving task performance across open agent ecosystems. The framework is evaluated on interactive environments ALFWorld and WebArena, showing that retrieving trajectories from MATM improves downstream task performance and reduces interaction steps.

MATM extends RAG to multi-agent systems by treating agent-generated trajectories as reusable artifacts, similar to how search engines index human-generated content.

## [ENPIRE: Autonomous Robot Policy Improvement in Real World](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19980) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NVIDIA researchers introduced ENPIRE, a harness framework that enables coding agents to autonomously improve robotic policies in the real world through a closed-loop feedback cycle of scene reset, policy execution, outcome verification, and refinement. This framework addresses a key bottleneck in robotics—the heavy reliance on human supervision for real-world policy improvement—and demonstrates that coding agents can autonomously achieve high success rates on dexterous manipulation tasks, potentially accelerating robotics research and deployment. ENPIRE consists of four modules: Environment (EN) for automatic reset and verification, Policy Improvement (PI) for launching refinements, Rollout (R) for parallel robot evaluation, and Evolution (E) where coding agents analyze logs and improve code. The framework achieved 99% success on tasks like organizing a pin box and fastening a zip tie.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Dexterous robotic manipulation in the real world typically requires extensive human supervision and algorithm engineering, which limits scalability. ENPIRE provides this missing abstraction by creating a closed-loop system that minimizes human effort.

## [Scale Dominates Multi-Agent Orchestration Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20058) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper evaluates DAG Plan and Execute and ReAct architectures across 208 enterprise scenarios at scales up to 200 agents, finding that scale—not task complexity—is the primary performance bottleneck due to agent discovery noise. This research addresses a critical gap in multi-agent orchestration for enterprise AI, providing empirical evidence that scaling degrades performance more than task complexity, which is vital for practitioners designing large-scale agent systems. The Task Manager introduced in the paper reduces high-priority queue latency by 14-75% and improves related-event correctness by over 20 percentage points at enterprise scale. DAG Plan and Execute offers higher precision at small scales but suffers higher overhead at enterprise scale, while ReAct is more robust due to incremental failure handling.

## [Lean as Process Oracle for Theorem Proving RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20068) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes using the Lean proof assistant as a symbolic process oracle to provide dense, tactic-level verified feedback for reinforcement learning in theorem proving, integrating this into a GRPO-style objective with first-error propagation. This approach bridges the gap between binary verification signals and rich process feedback, potentially improving automated theorem proving performance and enabling more reliable AI reasoning systems. The method parses proof attempts into tactic sequences, uses Lean's elaboration to mark sound steps and the earliest failing step, and applies first-token credit methods; experiments on STP-Lean and DeepSeek-Prover-V1.5 show improvements on MiniF2F and ProofNet benchmarks.

ScaffoldAgent proposes a utility-guided dynamic outline optimization framework for open-ended deep research, enabling controlled updates to report scaffolds via expansion, contraction, and revision operations. This framework addresses the critical problem of scaffold drift in long-form report generation, where outlines become misaligned as new information accumulates, potentially improving the quality and factual grounding of AI-generated research reports. ## [New Metric RVS Measures Logical Compliance of ML Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20208) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce the Rule Violation Score (RVS), a novel evaluation metric that quantifies how well predictive models adhere to logical rules, complementing traditional accuracy metrics. In high-stakes domains like healthcare and finance, logical consistency is as critical as accuracy, yet no standard metric existed; RVS fills this gap, enabling more reliable model evaluation. RVS treats hard rules (strict constraints) and soft rules (statistical regularities) differently, can be computed via automatically generated SQL queries for Horn rules, and works on any dataset and predictive model expressed over a relational vocabulary.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional machine learning evaluation focuses on predictive accuracy, such as classification error or ranking quality, but ignores whether outputs violate domain-specific logical constraints. In high-stakes applications, a model with high accuracy may still make logically inconsistent predictions, leading to unsafe decisions. RVS addresses this by providing a complementary metric that measures logical compliance independently of accuracy.

## [UFP4: Uniform FP4 Recipe Fixes Shrinkage Bias in LLM Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20381) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper identifies shrinkage bias in non-uniform FP4 formats like E2M1 for LLM pretraining and proposes UFP4, a uniform 4-bit recipe using E1M2/INT4 grids that eliminates this bias and improves training stability. This finding challenges the current hardware trend (e.g., NVIDIA Blackwell, AMD MI350) centered on E2M1, suggesting that future accelerators should support uniform 4-bit grids to achieve more stable and efficient LLM pretraining. The shrinkage bias accumulates multiplicatively across layers and is amplified by the Random Hadamard Transform (RHT), while uniform grids (E1M2/INT4) bypass this error. UFP4 applies RHT to all three training GEMMs and restricts stochastic rounding to dY alone, achieving lower BF16-relative loss degradation on models up to 124B parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: FP4 training reduces memory and computation for LLM pretraining, but current hardware (e.g., NVIDIA Blackwell) uses non-uniform E2M1 format. The Random Hadamard Transform (RHT) is a fast orthogonal transform used to improve quantization quality, but it can amplify certain biases.

## [Positional Bias in Diffusion LLMs: Analysis and Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19349) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that query position significantly affects in-context learning in diffusion LLMs due to a spatial recency effect in attention, and proposes Average Confidence and Auto-ICL to mitigate this bias. This work identifies a critical yet overlooked factor in diffusion LLMs, which are emerging as alternatives to autoregressive models, and provides practical solutions to improve their reliability and performance. The authors empirically show that positional variance impacts generation quality on par with example semantic quality, and that traditional single-step confidence fails in dLLMs, leading to the proposal of Average Confidence.

**Background**: In-context learning (ICL) allows LLMs to perform tasks by providing examples in the prompt without fine-tuning. Diffusion LLMs (dLLMs) use bidirectional attention and iterative denoising, unlike autoregressive models with causal masking. Current ICL practices for dLLMs often inherit trailing-query templates from AR models, which may be suboptimal.

## [Argent Signaling Protocol Mitigates Semantic Drift in Multi-Agent LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19356) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Argent Signaling Protocol (ASP), a machine-readable header that appends structured quality signals (certainty, grounding, stochasticity, assumption index) to every AI-generated response, enabling a controller to distinguish repairable failures from containment failures in multi-agent LLM systems. This protocol addresses a critical limitation in multi-agent LLM systems—semantic drift—by providing a principled way to decide whether to retry or halt, improving trustworthiness and reliability. It could enable safer deployment of autonomous AI agents in high-stakes domains like legal document analysis. In standalone mode on a 27-question document-grounded QA benchmark, ASP improved pass rate from 11.1% to 33.3% on Qwen (0.8B) and from 33.3% to 44.4% on Dobby (8B). In multi-agent mode, an ASP sidecar blocked 100% of ungrounded upstream outputs (24/27 blocked, 0 ungrounded propagations).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems often suffer from semantic drift, where agent outputs diverge from their original intent while remaining syntactically valid. Current retry strategies treat all failures identically, making it impossible for human supervisors to know whether a retry was warranted or the system should have halted. The Argent Signaling Protocol introduces structured quality signals to classify failures as repairable (grounded but incomplete) or containment (ungrounded), enabling appropriate routing.

## [Computational Identifiability: Bridging Theory and Practice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19361) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces 'computational identifiability,' a framework that replaces the idealized theoretical identifiability with a finite computational search procedure for empirical estimators, allowing identification with finite samples and ambiguous graphical criteria. This distinction addresses a critical gap in causal inference, making identification practical for real-world scenarios where theoretical assumptions (e.g., infinite data) are unrealistic. It could influence how researchers and practitioners approach causal effect estimation in machine learning and statistics. The framework requires a meta-prior over parameters and a hypothesis space of estimators (e.g., neural processes). Experiments show it works with small finite samples, ambiguous graphical criteria, mixed observational-interventional data, and counterfactual estimands.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: In causal inference, identifiability traditionally asks whether a causal effect can be uniquely determined from the causal graph and observational data, assuming infinite samples. This theoretical notion often fails in practice due to finite data or ambiguous graphs. Computational identifiability relaxes these idealizations by defining a finite search procedure that finds an estimator within a desired error tolerance, conditional on a prior and search procedure.

## [Wall-Clock Calibration Bistable in Agent Monitors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19386) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper corrects a prior finding on the State Saturation Trap in runtime monitors for autonomous agents, revealing that the trap is actually a calibration artifact: wall-clock-calibrated leaky integrators become bistable (constant alarm or silent) at agent cadence, while sample-time CUSUM remains dt-invariant. This finding has significant implications for AI safety and agent behavior analysis, as it shows that wall-clock-calibrated monitors cannot serve as moment detectors on agent streams, potentially leading to false alarms or missed transitions. It underscores the critical importance of calibration choice in runtime monitoring systems. The paper uses a pre-registered sweep over uniform intervals (dt from 0 to 600 seconds) on 20 trajectories, showing that wall-clock level triggers have two regimes: constant alarm at dt≤1s and silent at dt≥60s, with the critical region in (1,30]s. Real agent runs have median latency 1.53s, placing them inside the trap regime.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime monitors for autonomous agents often use threshold-on-state triggers, such as cumulative sum (CUSUM) or exponential moving average (EMA), to detect behavioral anomalies. The key distinction is whether the monitor's dynamics are calibrated in sample time (per observation) or wall-clock time (real seconds). On agent streams, inter-action times vary by orders of magnitude, causing these calibrations to diverge significantly.

The model conceptualizes security risk as a longitudinal behavioral phenomenon arising from human-AI interaction, unlike prior work focusing on static vulnerabilities. The gamified framework embeds adversarial evaluation, behavioral feedback, and security scoring into development workflows.

**Background**: Post-Quantum Cryptography (PQC) refers to cryptographic algorithms designed to resist attacks from quantum computers, but their implementation requires strict adherence to constant-time execution and side-channel resistance to avoid timing attacks. ## [In-Context Learning for Intrinsic Curiosity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19476) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes using in-context learning as an update-free world model to compute intrinsic curiosity rewards, avoiding expensive gradient updates. It proves that unbiased estimation is impossible in general MDPs but achievable in non-temporal settings like active learning. This work addresses a key scalability bottleneck in intrinsic curiosity for reinforcement learning, potentially enabling more efficient exploration in large-scale systems. It bridges in-context learning and exploration, opening new directions for sequence model-based agents. The authors prove that in general Markov decision processes, in-context learning-derived intrinsic rewards either suffer from nuisance terms or cannot be implemented. However, for non-temporal settings (e.g., Bayesian experimental design), the rewards bound and asymptotically converge to true learning progress.

**Background**: In-context learning (ICL) allows sequence models to adapt to new tasks via prompt examples without weight updates. Researchers introduced IHBench, a benchmark that evaluates how well voice agents recover from interruptions during structured, multi-step workflows across 10 enterprise domains. It tests 27 audio-language model configurations from OpenAI, Google, and open-weight communities on six interruption types. Existing benchmarks focus on interruption timing (e.g., barge-in), but ignore post-interruption recovery, which is critical for real-world voice agents in customer service, healthcare, and enterprise applications. This benchmark fills that gap and reveals that closed-weight models are significantly more robust than open-weight ones. The benchmark uses state-machine-driven workflows and generates per-interruption evaluation rubrics scoring task fulfillment and recovery quality. Closed-weight models degrade roughly 3.3x slower as conversations lengthen and show no audio-versus-text modality gap, while open-weight models lose ground on all three metrics.

The Lie bracket of vector fields measures the failure of flows to commute, and Frobenius residuals quantify this failure, which can indicate latent structure.

Researchers present the first end-to-end framework for formally verifying learned multi-agent communication policies by distilling neural policies into interpretable decision trees and verifying safety properties that transfer back to the original networks. The framework achieves 97.9% fidelity in decision tree distillation, verifies 18 temporal logic properties with 88.9% satisfaction, and ensures all five safety thresholds are met (e.g., 0.3% collision probability vs. 1% threshold). ## [SafeSpec: Fast and Safe LLM via Dynamic Reflective Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19755) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SafeSpec integrates a lightweight safety head into the target model's verification step, enabling joint evaluation of semantic validity and safety in a single forward pass during speculative decoding. This addresses a critical gap where existing safety methods are incompatible with speculative decoding, often negating its speed benefits. SafeSpec achieves a 2.06x speedup on benign workloads while reducing attack success rates by 15% on Qwen3-32B, showing that safety and efficiency can be jointly optimized. SafeSpec models jailbreak attacks as distributional shifts over generative trajectories and uses rollback with safety-guided reflective multi-sampling to recover safe continuations instead of terminating generation. The framework is evaluated across multiple models and adversarial benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by using a small draft model to generate candidate tokens, which are then verified in parallel by a large target model. However, this process lacks inherent safety guarantees, and existing safety defenses either add extra computation or disrupt the draft-verify mechanism, negating the speedup. Jailbreak attacks are adversarial prompts designed to elicit harmful responses from LLMs, posing a significant security risk.

Researchers propose Uncertainty-Aware Reward Modeling (UARM), which equips reward models with calibrated uncertainty via quantile-based conformal prediction and reweights GRPO advantages through heteroscedastic variance decomposition to mitigate reward hacking in RLHF. Researchers propose Frequency-Aware Flow Matching (FAFM), which uses discrete cosine transform (DCT) to convert discrete action sequences into the frequency domain for flow matching, enabling continuous and temporally consistent robotic action generation from heterogeneous-frequency demonstrations. FAFM addresses key limitations in robotic action generation—heterogeneous control frequencies and temporal inconsistency—without adding network parameters, improving success rates, motion smoothness, and robustness across multiple benchmarks and real-world robots. rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a generative modeling paradigm that learns to transform noise into data distributions via continuous flows, similar to diffusion models. In robotics, action generation often relies on discretized action chunks, which struggle with varying control frequencies and can produce jerky motions. The discrete cosine transform (DCT) is a technique that represents a signal as a sum of cosine functions at different frequencies, commonly used in image compression like JPEG.

SPOT-E introduces a test-time entropy shaping method that uses low-entropy anchors and GRPO-based optimization to generate visual spotlights, improving visual grounding in frozen vision-language models without retraining. This method addresses a key limitation in VLMs—overlooking small but decisive visual evidence—by providing a plug-and-play solution that enhances grounding and robustness, with potential impact on evidence-intensive tasks like medical imaging or autonomous driving. SPOT-E uses answer-span prediction entropy as a feedback signal, but avoids naive entropy minimization by preserving baseline high-confidence tokens via low-entropy anchors. It optimizes spotlight parameters per instance using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) in a short test-time episode.

Researchers propose a training-free structural compression pipeline that reduces the depth of Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models by up to 50% by removing redundant layers identified via Centered Kernel Alignment (CKA). This method significantly reduces training time (40-50%) and real-time inference latency (up to 30%) for VLA models, making them more practical for robotic manipulation tasks on resource-constrained hardware. The pipeline uses a single forward pass with CKA to measure layer similarity and removes twin layers, compressing both the VLM backbone and the continuous control policy head. It was validated on three simulation benchmarks and 10 real-world tasks across four robotic embodiments.

This work addresses a critical bottleneck for serving context-heavy AI agents, where long prefixes are reused across many short turns, by significantly reducing memory and latency. UltraQuant uses asymmetric K/V treatment, Walsh-Hadamard rotation instead of random rotation, removes QJL bias correction, and employs block-scale variants. It introduces an FP4 approximation path with FP8 queries, FP4 KV tensors, UE8M0 group scales, and native scaled-MFMA support on CDNA4.

Rotation-based methods like TurboQuant use random or Hadamard rotations to decorrelate vectors before quantization, improving accuracy.

This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent security by extending runtime monitoring to probabilistic policies, enabling enforcement of security policies under ambiguity without requiring independence assumptions, which is essential for real-world applications like PII detection. The framework outperforms prior art on standard benchmarks for terminal and tool calling agents, improving the security-utility trade-off while ensuring rigorous probabilistic bounds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime monitoring for AI agents typically uses deterministic policies expressed in Datalog. However, many real-world scenarios involve probabilistic predicates (e.g., a PII detector with failure probability) and correlations between them, which prior probabilistic inference methods for Datalog cannot handle without independence assumptions. Distributionally robust optimization (DRO) is a technique that optimizes under worst-case distributions, providing robustness against ambiguity.

## [Sovereign Execution Brokers Enforce Certificate-Bound Authority for Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20520) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Sovereign Execution Broker (SEB), a runtime enforcement boundary that verifies certificate-bound authority before allowing autonomous agents to execute mutations on infrastructure APIs. This addresses a critical security gap in agentic systems by ensuring that production mutation authority is not embedded inside non-deterministic reasoning processes, providing a mandatory enforcement point for certified authority at the moment of mutation. SEB consumes certificates from the Sovereign Assurance Boundary (SAB), verifies execution contracts, checks validity windows, policy epochs, revocation epochs, and live-state drift, then mints scoped execution identity before invoking infrastructure APIs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous agents increasingly control cloud and deployment workflows, but existing access control authorizes identities while assurance layers certify actions, lacking a unified enforcement point at mutation time. SEB separates proposal, admission, and execution to turn certified authority into a short-lived, revocable, auditable runtime capability.

Researchers released SARLO-80, a worldwide dataset of 119,566 triplets pairing very-high-resolution SAR SLC imagery (standardized to 80cm slant-range) with aligned optical tiles and natural-language captions, built from Umbra spotlight SICD data covering 257 locations across 72 countries. This dataset fills a critical gap in multimodal foundation models for SAR by providing large-scale, physically grounded data that preserves complex-valued measurements and native acquisition geometry, enabling reproducible benchmarks for cross-modal retrieval and conditional generation. All SAR data are resampled to an 80cm slant-range grid via band-limited FFT resampling, and optical tiles are warped into the SAR grid using local coordinate correspondences for pixel-level alignment. ## [Deception Detection Needs Targeted Probes, Not Universal Ones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01425) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that linear probes for detecting AI deception are heterogeneous, requiring type-specific probes for optimal performance, challenging the universal probe approach. The study shows that matching probes to specific deception types yields up to +0.108 AUC improvement over a single universal probe. This finding is critical for AI safety because it reveals that a one-size-fits-all deception detector is insufficient, forcing organizations to define specific threat models and deploy matched probes. It highlights the need for more nuanced monitoring strategies as AI systems become more capable. The paper found that instruction pairs capture deceptive intent rather than content-specific patterns, with prompt choice dominating 70.6% of probe performance variance. Synthetic validation experiments suggest the theoretical ceiling of +0.108 AUC improvement is achievable a priori when the deception type is known.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Linear probes are classifiers trained on internal model activations to detect specific behaviors, such as deception. Previous work showed that a single linear probe trained on a contrastive instruction pair could detect deception with moderate success, but this paper reveals that deception is heterogeneous and requires type-specific probes.

## [Hard Constraint Diffusion via Doob's h-Transform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05533) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a conditional diffusion guidance framework that enforces hard constraints with probability one using Doob's h-transform and martingale theory, and proposes two off-policy learning algorithms to estimate the conditioning function. This work provides a principled solution for safety-critical AI applications where constraint satisfaction must be guaranteed, and it also enables rare-event simulation without modifying the pretrained diffusion model. The framework adds a drift correction term involving the logarithmic gradient of a conditioning function, estimated via a martingale loss and a martingale-covariation loss using only trajectories from the pretrained model. Non-asymptotic guarantees are provided in total variation and Wasserstein distances.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by reversing a noising process, but standard conditional generation methods (e.g., classifier guidance) provide only soft constraints. Hard constraints require the sample to satisfy a condition with probability one, which is essential in safety-critical domains. Doob's h-transform is a stochastic process technique that conditions a Markov process to hit a target set, and martingale theory provides tools to analyze such conditioned processes.

## [Decoupled Prover-Verifier Game Reduces Legibility Tax](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23248) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a decoupled prover-verifier game (DPVG) that trains a separate translator model to convert a fixed solver's outputs into a checkable form, eliminating the accuracy degradation (legibility tax) seen in standard prover-verifier games. By removing the legibility tax, DPVG enables LLMs to produce both accurate and easily verifiable outputs, which is crucial for AI safety and interpretability in high-stakes applications. The decoupled approach first trains a solver model solely for correctness, then trains a translator to make the solver's output checkable while preserving the answer. The game's equilibria correspond to faithful and checkable translators.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Prover-verifier games are interactive proof systems where a prover tries to convince a verifier of a statement's truth. In LLM training, they improve output legibility but often reduce accuracy, a phenomenon known as the legibility tax. This paper addresses that trade-off.

## [Scaffold Effect: Prompt Framing Drives Apparent VLM Gains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28387) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that up to 80% of performance gains in clinical vision-language models (VLMs) are due to prompt framing rather than genuine multimodal reasoning, coining the term 'scaffold effect'. This finding challenges the validity of current VLM evaluations in medicine, highlighting that apparent multimodal gains may be artifacts, which has direct implications for deploying trustworthy AI in clinical settings. The study evaluated 12 open-weight VLMs on two clinical neuroimaging datasets where MRI data carries no diagnostic signal, and found that merely mentioning MRI in the prompt accounts for 70-80% of F1 score improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both images and text, often used in medical imaging to combine visual data with clinical notes. Modality collapse occurs when a model ignores one modality (e.g., images) and relies solely on text, yet still appears to perform multimodal reasoning. The 'scaffold effect' is a domain-specific instance of modality collapse where prompt framing alone creates the illusion of evidence integration.

## [CogniFold: Always-On Proactive Memory via Cognitive Folding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CogniFold introduces a brain-inspired memory system that continuously organizes fragmented events into self-emerging cognitive structures, extending Complementary Learning Systems (CLS) theory with a prefrontal intent layer. This work addresses a key limitation of current agent memory—its reactive and retrieval-based nature—by enabling proactive, autonomous memory organization, which could significantly advance the development of truly autonomous AI agents. CogniFold uses graph-topology self-organization: cognitive structures merge when semantically similar, decay when stale, relink via associative recall, and surface intents when concept-cluster density crosses a threshold. It is evaluated on CogEval-Bench and eight downstream benchmarks including LoCoMo and LongMemEval.

**Background**: Complementary Learning Systems (CLS) theory posits that intelligent agents need two learning systems: a fast-learning hippocampus for specific experiences and a slow-learning neocortex for general knowledge. CogniFold extends this by adding a prefrontal intent layer, emulating the prefrontal cortex's role in intentional control and decision-making.

## [STAR: Spatiotemporal Adaptive Reward for Text-to-Image RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17979) ⭐️ 8.0/10

STAR introduces a spatiotemporal adaptive reward allocation method for RL post-training of text-to-image diffusion and flow models, using text-image attention to dynamically assign rewards to relevant latent regions across denoising steps. This addresses a fundamental granularity mismatch in existing RL post-training methods, enabling more precise policy updates that focus on generative components affecting the reward, which could significantly improve text-image alignment and generation quality. STAR uses Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium as the base model and achieves state-of-the-art scores of 0.9759 on GenEval, 0.9757 on OCR text rendering, and 23.60 on PickScore, with almost no additional computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training is a standard technique for fine-tuning text-to-image diffusion models to improve aspects like image quality and prompt alignment. Existing methods typically convert the final-image reward into a single scalar advantage applied uniformly across the entire generative trajectory, ignoring the temporal and spatial structure of the generation process.

## [TxBench-PP: New Benchmark Tests AI Agents on Drug Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19245) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced TxBench-PP, a verifiable benchmark for evaluating AI agents on small-molecule preclinical pharmacology tasks using real-world assay data. The benchmark includes 100 evaluations across mechanism-of-action, pharmacodynamic reasoning, and other key areas, and tests agents by having them inspect files in a coding environment and return structured answers. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in realistic preclinical pharmacology assessment for AI agents, which could accelerate AI-driven drug development. The finding that no current AI system reliably recovers preclinical pharmacology decisions highlights the need for further progress in this high-impact domain. The strongest configuration, Claude Opus 4.8 with Pi, passed only 59.3% of endpoint attempts (178/300), followed by GPT-5.5 with Pi at 55.3%. The benchmark spans 16 model-harness configurations, 11 models, and 4,800 trajectories, covering tasks such as compound-target engagement, causal target validation, developability and safety, and translational efficacy.

**Background**: Small-molecule drugs are compounds with low molecular weight (typically <900 Daltons) that interact with specific targets to produce therapeutic effects. AI agents are increasingly used to accelerate drug discovery, but their performance on realistic preclinical tasks has been difficult to evaluate due to a lack of standardized benchmarks.

## [DiverseDistill: Distilling from Foundation Models and Domain Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14035) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose DiverseDistill, a framework that uses a diverse committee of domain experts and foundation models with a learnable QA mechanism to improve knowledge distillation to compact models. This addresses a key challenge in distilling from large foundation models to compact domain models, where naive multi-teacher methods often degrade performance. DiverseDistill recovers 73-114% of the teacher-student performance gap on recommendation and vision tasks, enabling efficient deployment of powerful models on resource-constrained devices. DiverseDistill uses frozen teachers with only forward-pass inference through intermediate layers, requiring no parameter updates or co-training. A dynamic teacher importance mechanism reduces training cost by filtering low-relevance teachers per sample (e.g., ~30% fewer forward passes for recommendation tasks), and the distillation module is discarded after training, adding zero inference overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation is a technique where a compact student model learns from a larger teacher model to achieve similar performance with lower computational cost. Foundation models are large-scale models trained on broad data, while domain experts are smaller models specialized for specific tasks. Multi-teacher distillation combines knowledge from multiple teachers, but naively combining heterogeneous teachers can degrade performance due to output misalignment.

This work moves beyond informal analogies to provide a structured, mathematically grounded framework, potentially accelerating progress in both distribution shift robustness and AI safety research. The paper identifies two connection types: (1) methods for a shift type can help achieve a safety goal, and (2) certain shifts and safety issues can be formally reduced to each other. RoboSSM replaces Transformers with Longhorn, a state-of-the-art state-space model (SSM), for in-context imitation learning (ICIL), achieving linear-time inference and improved generalization to unseen and long-horizon tasks on the LIBERO benchmark. ## [Mitigating General-Capability Forgetting in Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21978) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper identifies that reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) causes large reasoning models to forget foundational skills like perception and faithfulness, and proposes RECAP, a replay strategy with dynamic objective reweighting to mitigate this catastrophic forgetting. This work addresses a critical and underexplored issue in RLVR post-training, which is widely used for improving reasoning in LLMs. If unaddressed, capability regression could undermine model safety and reliability, making this research timely for AI alignment and robustness. The paper empirically demonstrates performance degradation on core capabilities such as perception and faithfulness in open-source reasoning models. The proposed RECAP method uses online short-horizon signals of convergence and instability to dynamically reweight objectives, and it is end-to-end without requiring additional models or heavy tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a post-training paradigm where models receive rewards based on automatically checkable criteria (e.g., correct math answer). Catastrophic forgetting occurs when learning new tasks overwrites previously acquired knowledge, a known challenge in LLM fine-tuning. Experience replay is a technique that stores past experiences to mitigate forgetting, but deciding how to weight different objectives remains nontrivial.

PAVE minimizes Q-gradient volatility while preserving local curvature, achieving smoothness comparable to policy-side regularization methods with competitive task performance.

LoRDO is a new framework that combines low-rank optimization with infrequent synchronization for distributed training of foundation models, achieving near-parity with DDP while reducing communication by about 10x. This addresses a key bottleneck in distributed training—interconnect bandwidth and memory—by reducing both communication and optimizer state memory, making large-scale training more efficient and accessible. LoRDO introduces a full-rank quasi-hyperbolic update to restore subspace exploration, overcoming the limitation of permanent low-rank subspace restriction from global projections based on pseudo-gradients.

Infrequent communication strategies reduce synchronization frequency but still require optimizer state communication.

## [Defense Training Breaks LLM Agents While Failing Against Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19423) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that defense training designed to protect LLM agents from prompt injection paradoxically destroys their competence on benign tasks while failing to stop sophisticated attacks, introducing three systematic biases: agent incompetence, cascade amplification, and trigger bias. This finding exposes a fundamental capability-alignment paradox in LLM agent safety, showing that current defense paradigms optimize for single-turn refusal benchmarks while rendering multi-step agents unreliable, which has critical implications for deploying autonomous AI systems in real-world applications. The study evaluated defended models against undefended baselines across 97 agent tasks and 1,000 adversarial prompts, finding that defended models timeout on 99% of tasks compared to 13% for baselines, and that straightforward attacks bypass defenses at high rates due to shortcut learning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents use external tools like file operations and API calls to complete multi-step tasks autonomously. Prompt injection attacks manipulate agent behavior through malicious observations or retrieved content, and defense training aims to make agents resistant to such attacks. However, this study shows that defense training introduces biases that break agent functionality.

## [RL Foundation Models: A New Frontier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18812) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper argues that reinforcement learning (RL) lacks foundation models because prior design for MDPs has not been treated as a primary objective, and proposes that MDPs' sufficient statistics make them amenable to tabular foundation model architectures like TabPFN. As a proof of concept, the authors train a Graph Attention Network on synthetic MDPs that solves held-out tabular benchmarks in context, both online and offline. This work identifies a significant gap in RL research and proposes a concrete path toward RL foundation models, potentially enabling in-context RL agents that generalize across tasks without task-specific tuning. If successful, it could parallel the impact of foundation models in NLP and vision, accelerating RL applications in robotics, game playing, and decision-making. The paper shows that MDPs admit a fixed-size sufficient statistic independent of episode count, making them tabular in shape and directly compatible with attention-based architectures used for tabular foundation models. The proof-of-concept Graph Attention Network, trained entirely on synthetic MDPs, outperforms UCB-VI and tabular Q-learning online and matches VI-LCB offline, all without task-specific tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models like GPT-4 and TabPFN are pretrained on large-scale data and can be adapted to many tasks without retraining. TabPFN, a transformer pretrained on synthetic Bayesian priors, excels at tabular classification and regression. In-context reinforcement learning (ICRL) aims to adapt RL agents to new tasks via prompt conditioning, but prior work has not systematically designed priors for MDPs. This paper proposes that MDPs' sufficient statistics enable a tabular foundation model approach for RL.

<a id="item-171"></a>
## [TRAP Benchmark: Balancing Task Accuracy and Privacy in AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18996) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced TRAP (Task-completion and Resistance to Active Privacy-extraction), a benchmark that evaluates the trade-off between task accuracy and resistance to privacy extraction in AI agents handling sensitive documents. Testing 22 models, they found all exhibit non-trivial privacy leakage, and instruction-following ability correlates with leakage rate. This benchmark addresses a critical tension in AI agent deployment: agents must use private information to complete tasks but must never expose it. The findings highlight fundamental limitations of prompt-based defenses and propose a structural solution, which could influence the design of privacy-preserving AI systems. The impossibility result shows that for any softmax-based model, no soft-constraint defense (e.g., prompt-based defenses) can jointly achieve high task success with zero leakage probability. The proposed defense, structural private field isolation, replaces private fields with hash keys before the model processes them, largely preventing leakage while maintaining task accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly used in document-intensive workflows where sensitive private information is routine, such as booking flights with passport numbers. These agents must use private data to complete tasks but cannot verify who is at the keyboard, creating a tension between task completion and privacy. Existing prompt-based defenses attempt to instruct the model not to reveal private information, but the paper proves such defenses are fundamentally insufficient for softmax-based models.

CSSQuake is a browser-based recreation of the classic game Quake, where the entire 3D rendering is done using CSS transforms and styles, powered by the PolyCSS engine. ## [New Pipeline Measures CS Curriculum Alignment with Guidelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19469) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed a human-in-the-loop pipeline to measure how well a computer science program covers curricular guidelines, applied longitudinally to CS2013 and CS2023. The pipeline uses semantic retrieval and human judgment to map courses to knowledge units, with a reciprocal-rank-fusion ensemble outperforming a long-context model. This provides a reproducible, validated method for programs to assess their alignment with evolving curricular standards, helping educators identify persistent gaps and adapt to raised expectations. It also benchmarks retrieval models for curriculum alignment, showing that smaller models can outperform larger ones. The pipeline was tested on one accredited BSc program, finding 49.7% coverage of CS2023 and 50.9% of CS2013 knowledge units. Competency articulation was ~88% for covered units under both guidelines, but cognitive depth delivery dropped from 95% under CS2013 to 76% under CS2023, reflecting the newer guideline's higher expectations.

REVEAL++ introduces a continuous, differentiable formulation of phenotypic similarity for contrastive learning in retinal image analysis, replacing discrete group assignments with soft multi-positive relationships to improve Alzheimer's disease risk prediction. This approach enables end-to-end learning of phenotypic structure and cross-modal alignment, potentially leading to more accurate and scalable neurodegenerative risk modeling from retinal imaging and clinical data. The method models inter-subject similarity as a differentiable weighting function derived from intra-modality embedding similarities, and introduces a soft-target contrastive objective. Evaluated on UK Biobank data for incident AD prediction, it outperforms discrete group-based contrastive learning and standard vision-language baselines.

Researchers propose a prompt-based method to decompose uncertainty into action confidence and request uncertainty, enabling LLM agents to proactively ask for clarification when tasks are underspecified. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent uncertainty quantification by providing a practical, prompt-based solution that works under real-world constraints like black-box APIs and latency budgets. It enables agents to handle ambiguous instructions more effectively, improving reliability in interactive systems. The method improves clarification F1 on ALFWorld-Clarification by 73% over ReAct+UE and 36% over UAM, averaged across five LLM backbones (GPT-5.1, DeepSeek-v3.2-exp, GLM-4.7, Qwen3.5-35B, GPT-OSS-120B). ## [DIF: Denoising Implicit Feedback for Cold-Start Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose DIF, a model-agnostic denoising method that uses stable user preferences from warm items to generate pseudo-labels for cold-start items, improving recommendation accuracy. Cold-start items suffer from noisy implicit feedback (e.g., clickbait), and existing denoising methods fail in such scenarios. DIF addresses this gap and has been deployed in Kuaishou, a billion-user short video app, significantly improving commercial metrics. DIF infers pseudo-labels by leveraging content similarity between cold and warm items, models confidence based on similarity, and aggregates multiple pseudo-labels. It also uses relative entropy and cold-start status to estimate label uncertainty and adaptively correct noisy labels.

This work bridges cooperative and noncooperative game theory, offering a rigorous framework for distributed multi-agent coordination with potential applications in AI and economics. The dynamics use the Aumann-Dreze value to compute payoffs within the current coalition, and the paper establishes conditions for Lyapunov and potential function representations, as well as the impact of switching and acceptance costs on local stability.

## [SEVRA: Selective Verification for Budget-Aware LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19808) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SEVRA (Selective Verification for Reasoning Allocation), a serving-layer controller that decides whether to keep a frozen solver's initial answer or invoke active verification, reducing compute waste and harmful answer flips while maintaining or improving accuracy. This work addresses a critical deployment problem for LLMs: extra reasoning is not uniformly valuable and can waste compute or introduce errors. SEVRA offers a practical, budget-aware solution that improves efficiency and reliability, which is important for real-world AI systems where compute cost and answer stability matter. On the MATH-500 benchmark, SEVRA achieved 76.3% accuracy vs. 75.5% for always verifying, while reducing post-generation tokens by 26.8% and harmful flips from 2.2% to 1.0%. However, a longer initial solve (8,192 tokens) reached 76.0% accuracy with 28% fewer total tokens, showing that selective recovery is useful but not the best cost frontier.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time reasoning is increasingly used as a control knob for LLM serving, but extra computation can be wasteful or harmful. SEVRA treats this as a deployment allocation problem, using recoverability-aware gates trained from serving-visible attempt state to decide when to verify. The paper compares selective verification against simply allocating more tokens to the initial solve, providing a practical deployment rule: tune the initial budget first, then use selective recovery when explicit checks or auditability matter.

## [eCNNTO: CNN with Residual Connections Accelerates Topology Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes eCNNTO, a convolutional neural network with residual connections that predicts near-optimal densities from early iteration history, achieving up to 90% iteration reduction in 2D and 97% in 3D topology optimization problems. This work significantly accelerates density-based topology optimization, a key tool in aerospace and automotive design, by reducing computational cost while maintaining design quality. It also demonstrates strong generalization to different boundary conditions, loading cases, and mesh resolutions. eCNNTO improves upon a prior Deep Belief Network (DBN) method by incorporating spatial correlations via CNN with residual connections, preventing disconnected features. A novel training strategy uses final-stage density histories instead of early ones, reducing required training data size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Topology optimization (TO) is a computational method that finds the optimal material layout within a given design space, often using finite element analysis (FEA) iteratively. Density-based TO represents material distribution as a density field, but requires many FEA iterations, especially for high-resolution meshes. Prior work used a DBN per element to predict densities, but lacked spatial coherence. eCNNTO addresses this by using a CNN that captures neighborhood information.

PhysDrift eliminates the generation-level embodiment gap by learning motion-space attraction dynamics in robot configuration space, producing smoother trajectories and enabling real-time one-step inference. This work challenges the conservative rollout paradigm in reinforcement learning for world models, enabling scalable exploration under complex dynamics while addressing reward hacking, which is a critical obstacle in RL-based world model refinement. The paper introduces residual-space evolutionary optimization, a framework that combines flow-based generative models with evolutionary algorithms to enable non-differentiable data editing via evolutionary search. This work addresses a key limitation of flow-based generative editing, which previously required differentiable objectives, and extends its applicability to black-box or non-differentiable tasks in scientific domains like crystal data analysis. ## [ModSync: Fixing Capacity-Induced Failure in PINNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20156) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies a capacity-induced failure mode in physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) where overparameterized networks develop task-exclusive modules, and proposes ModSync, a framework that integrates structural optimization into conflict-averse training to mitigate this issue. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in PINN training, showing that simply increasing model capacity can backfire due to functional modularity, and provides a practical solution that improves accuracy and training stability across diverse PDE benchmarks. ModSync penalizes task-exclusive connections while preserving interaction-promoting pathways, preventing the network from self-partitioning into modules that hinder convergence to Pareto-stationary points. The method achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on diverse PDE benchmarks, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) embed physical laws, such as partial differential equations (PDEs), into the loss function of a neural network. Conflict-averse optimization methods aim to reduce gradient interference between different loss terms (e.g., residual and boundary losses). Overparameterization, while often beneficial, can lead to functional modularity where different parts of the network specialize in different tasks, reducing cross-objective interaction.

## [SoftSkill: Compressing Agent Skills into Compact Context Vectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20333) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SoftSkill introduces a method to compress natural-language agent skills into compact continuous context vectors, using a frozen backbone model and a trainable soft delta. On Qwen3.5-4B, a length-32 SoftSkill prefix improves accuracy by 8.3 points on SearchQA, 42.1 points on LiveMath, and 1.3 points on DocVQA compared to no-skill prompting. This approach reduces the inference-time overhead of processing long Markdown skill files, enabling more efficient deployment of AI agents. It also suggests that task skills can be represented as compact latent controls rather than textual artifacts, potentially changing how agent behaviors are designed and transferred. SoftSkill uses a frozen backbone (Qwen3.5-4B) and only trains a small set of continuous vectors (soft skills) via next-token prediction. Compared to SkillOpt, SoftSkill improves accuracy by 5.2 points on SearchQA and 12.5 points on LiveMath, while replacing hundreds to thousands of Markdown tokens with just 32 virtual tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Agent skills are typically written as natural-language Markdown files that describe policies and procedures. At inference, a language model must read and interpret these long texts, which is computationally expensive. SoftSkill instead learns a compact continuous representation that can be prepended to the input, avoiding the need to reprocess the full skill text each time.

## [Hierarchical Bayesian Model Boosts IVF Prediction with Environmental Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20459) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed a hierarchical Bayesian Beta regression model that uses 55 context-aware temporal features from incubator environments to predict IVF pregnancy rates, achieving a prediction error of 1.27% and an R² of 0.86 across clinics. This work demonstrates that high-resolution environmental monitoring contains clinically meaningful, transferable signals for IVF outcomes, potentially improving success rates by optimizing lab conditions. It also showcases the value of hierarchical Bayesian methods in healthcare AI for sharing information across sites. The model uses partial pooling to share environmental effects across an Asian and a Northern European clinic while preserving site-specific baselines. For the 35-39 age group, it achieved a 64% error reduction over a naive baseline on held-out data from the Northern European clinic.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: In vitro fertilization (IVF) success is typically modeled using patient-level variables like age and hormone levels, while laboratory environmental conditions (e.g., temperature, humidity) are often summarized as raw averages. Hierarchical Bayesian modeling allows information to be shared across groups (e.g., clinics) via partial pooling, balancing group-specific effects with global trends. Context-aware temporal features capture dynamic patterns such as stability and recovery speed, which raw averages miss.

## [FlowEdit: Lifelong Pronunciation Adaptation for TTS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20518) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FlowEdit introduces a lifelong pronunciation adaptation framework for flow-matching text-to-speech (TTS) that stores corrections as latent edits in a Modern Hopfield Network, achieving a 92.7% reduction in Phoneme Error Rate without retraining. This approach addresses a critical limitation of static TTS models—persistent pronunciation errors on out-of-vocabulary proper nouns—without requiring costly retraining, enabling practical lifelong improvement in deployed systems. Corrections are learned as token-level perturbations in the text embedding space and stored in a Modern Hopfield Network for content-addressable retrieval with fuzzy morphological matching. Each correction takes about 15 seconds on a single GPU.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Flow-matching TTS models generate speech by mapping noise to acoustic features via time-dependent vector fields, achieving high zero-shot quality. However, they cannot correct pronunciation errors after deployment without retraining. Modern Hopfield Networks are associative memories with exponential storage capacity, enabling efficient pattern retrieval.
