## [DeepSWIP: Exact Counterfactuals for Neural Probabilistic Logic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20526) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DeepSWIP introduces a single-world counterfactual semantics for DeepProbLog programs, enabling exact counterfactual reasoning via weighted model counting over a single transformed program. This work bridges a gap in neurosymbolic AI by providing a causal semantics for counterfactual reasoning, which is crucial for explainability and robust decision-making in systems that combine neural perception with probabilistic logic. The method uses neural materialization to reduce neural predicates to ordinary ProbLog choices, and under finite grounding and unique-supported-model assumptions, it is exact relative to the learned materialized functional causal model. Experiments on MPI3D show a 2.14× inference speedup over the DeepTwin construction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: DeepProbLog is a neurosymbolic language that integrates deep learning with probabilistic logic programming, but its standard inference is associational and lacks causal semantics. Counterfactual reasoning requires modeling interventions and evidence, which is typically handled by twin networks but at the cost of duplicating the neural network. DeepSWIP avoids this duplication by using a single-world approach based on Single World Intervention Programs (SWIPs) and weighted model counting.

## [LedgerAgent: Structured State for Policy-Adherent Tool-Calling Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20529) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LedgerAgent introduces an inference-time method that maintains observed task states in a separate ledger and renders them into the prompt, also checking state-dependent policy constraints before executing environment-changing tool calls. This addresses a critical failure mode in tool-calling agents where implicit state management leads to stale information or policy violations, improving reliability in customer-service domains and beyond. Across four customer-service domains and a mixed panel of open- and closed-weight models, LedgerAgent improves average pass^k over standard prompt-based approaches, with largest gains under stricter multi-trial consistency metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-calling agents in customer-service domains must maintain task states (facts, identifiers, constraints) across turns while obeying domain policies. Standard agents do not represent task states separately, leading to failures from stale information or policy violations. LedgerAgent introduces a separate ledger to explicitly track and enforce state-dependent policies.

## [Cross-Attention Attribution Reveals How Style Captions Shape Speech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20532) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes cross-attention attribution for speech diffusion models, adapting the DAAM framework to the speech domain for the first time, and applies it to CapSpeech-TTS to analyze how style caption tokens influence acoustic output. This work provides the first interpretability analysis of how natural language instructions control voice characteristics in style-captioned TTS, which is crucial for diagnosing failure modes and improving controllability in expressive speech synthesis. The method extracts per-token heatmaps across 25 layers and 24 ODE steps, analyzing 3,600 (style caption, text transcript) combinations. Key findings include that style tokens have lower temporal variance, attention correlates with F0 and energy, and style conditioning peaks in early steps and deep layers.

Diffusion models are a class of generative models that iteratively denoise data to produce high-quality outputs. Cross-attention attribution maps, originally from the DAAM framework for image generation, help visualize which parts of an input influence the output.

## [MoE Calibration Under Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20544) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper studies calibration in mixture-of-experts (MoE) models under distribution shift, showing that expert calibration is sufficient for hard-routed models but not for soft-routed models, and proposes an adversarial reweighting method to improve calibration. This work addresses a gap in understanding how calibration interacts with routing mechanisms in MoE models under distribution shift, which is crucial for deploying reliable AI systems in real-world scenarios where data distributions often change. The proposed adversarial reweighting method penalizes calibration errors of the routed aggregate under distribution shift, and experiments show it improves the accuracy-calibration tradeoff across model classes, prediction tasks, and distribution shifts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Calibration in machine learning refers to aligning a model's predicted probabilities with the actual outcome frequencies. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) models use a routing mechanism to select or weight multiple expert sub-models for each input. Distribution shift occurs when the test data distribution differs from the training distribution, which can degrade model performance and calibration.

## [LUCID: Detecting Hallucinations in LLM-based KG Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19351) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose LUCID, the first hallucination detection method specifically designed for LLM-based knowledge graph reasoning, which jointly leverages LLM attention scores, KG semantics, and structural information via a graph neural network. Hallucinations in LLM-based KG reasoning can lead to misinformation and unreliable decisions; LUCID addresses this critical issue by integrating structural KG information overlooked by prior methods, potentially improving the reliability of AI systems in question answering, recommendation, and decision support. LUCID extracts node and edge features from LLM attention scores and semantic similarities, then integrates them with KG structure using a graph neural network. The method achieves state-of-the-art performance on nine benchmark datasets compared to 15 baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graph reasoning infers new knowledge from existing facts and is used in tasks like question answering and recommendation. Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for this purpose, but they often produce hallucinations—incorrect or nonsensical outputs—even when relevant KG knowledge is provided. Existing hallucination detection methods either analyze LLM internal states or verify consistency with retrieved contexts, but they ignore the structural information in KGs, which LUCID aims to exploit.

## [ILL Interpreted as PGM Structure Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19366) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper demonstrates that information lattice learning (ILL) can be interpreted as probabilistic graphical model (PGM) structure learning, where learned rules correspond to marginal constraints and lifting relates to maximum entropy. This connection provides a novel theoretical foundation for ILL, linking it to well-established PGM and maximum entropy frameworks, which could enhance interpretability and enable new hybrid symbolic-probabilistic learning methods. Under Shannon-entropy lifting, the constraints yield a log-linear factor graph whose factors are indexed by learned abstractions, but the information lattice itself is not a Bayesian network because its edges encode refinement/coarsening rather than conditional dependence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Information lattice learning (ILL) is a framework for learning interpretable rules from data by projecting onto a partition lattice and lifting selected rules. Probabilistic graphical models (PGMs) represent complex distributions using graphs encoding conditional dependencies. The maximum entropy principle selects the least committal distribution consistent with given constraints.

Researchers introduced cAPM, a continual learning framework for AI-assisted pace-mapping that transfers knowledge across multiple ventricular tachycardia cases, achieving 81% localization probability within 5 mm using only 4.5 pacing sites, compared to 38% with 13.7 sites for the state-of-the-art active-learning method. This work significantly improves the efficiency of pace-mapping, a critical step in catheter ablation for ventricular tachycardia, potentially reducing procedure time and improving patient outcomes. It demonstrates a novel application of continual learning in a clinical setting, paving the way for smarter AI-assisted interventions. cAPM uses a task-agnostic surrogate neural network to learn the mapping from pacing sites to 12-lead ECG morphology, combined with an active-learning strategy and continual learning to retain knowledge from prior targets. Evaluated on an in-silico testbed with sequentially-presented localization tasks across different physiological conditions and ventricular geometries.

## [Concept Flow Models: Hierarchical Bottlenecks for Better Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19489) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Concept Flow Models (CFMs), which replace the flat concept bottleneck in Concept Bottleneck Models with a hierarchical decision tree to reduce information leakage and improve interpretability. This addresses a critical limitation of Concept Bottleneck Models, where increasing concept count leads to information leakage and spurious correlations, undermining interpretability. CFMs offer a path toward more transparent and auditable AI reasoning. CFMs construct decision hierarchies from visual embeddings, distribute semantic concepts at each level, and train differentiable concept weights via probabilistic tree traversal. Experiments show CFMs match flat CBM performance while substantially reducing effective concept usage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) insert an intermediate layer of human-interpretable concepts before the final prediction, enabling concept-level intervention and debugging. However, when the number of concepts approaches the embedding dimension, information leakage occurs, allowing the model to exploit spurious correlations. Decision trees are inherently interpretable due to their hierarchical structure, which CFMs leverage to create stepwise decision flows.

This work addresses the critical challenge of cross-dataset generalization in audio deepfake detection, outperforming larger models like RawGAT-ST and Whisper-DF while matching SSL Wav2vec2 at 0.01% of its parameter count. ## [Co-policy: Real-Time Human-Robot Music Co-Creation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19914) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Co-policy, a framework that enables real-time human-robot musical co-creation by separating semantic intent grounding, constrained musical variation, and visuomotor execution using a fine-tuned Qwen-vl planner and a Gaussian-mixture visuomotor policy. This work bridges semantic musical understanding and physically executable robot actions, advancing embodied AI for creative domains and enabling robots to generate complementary musical responses rather than just playback. Co-policy uses a fine-tuned Qwen-vl planner (F-Qwen) to transform speech, live musical seeds, and visual observations into structured co-creation plans, and a Gaussian-Mixture Visuomotor Policy (GMP) for low-latency execution in a single forward pass.

**Background**: Human-robot co-creation in music requires connecting semantic musical understanding with real-time physically executable performance. Prior robotic playback systems merely reproduce user-specified notes, lacking the ability to generate complementary musical responses. Co-policy addresses this by separating intent grounding, variation, and execution.

## [STORM: Spatial-Aware Token Reduction for Vision Mamba](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19932) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose STORM, a spatial-aware token reduction framework that prevents performance collapse in Mamba-based vision models by preserving structural integrity during compression. This addresses a critical limitation in Mamba-based vision models, enabling efficient token reduction without sacrificing accuracy, which is essential for deploying these models in resource-constrained environments. STORM reformulates token reduction into a structured operation on spatial units, enforcing localized constraints to maintain grid topology and neighborhood coherence, and achieves state-of-the-art pruning accuracy under training-free settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Mamba is a state space model (SSM) that offers linear-time sequence modeling and has been adapted for vision tasks. However, existing token reduction methods are spatially agnostic, violating the 2D structural premise required by Mamba's selective scanning mechanism, leading to severe performance drops when applied to structurally enhanced Mamba variants.

Researchers propose SIMBA, a unified bidirectional retrieval-forward simulation framework with cycle-consistency and a Mamba state-space model for FY-4A GIIRS hyperspectral infrared radiance modeling in numerical weather prediction (NWP). This work addresses the limitation of existing deep learning methods that focus only on one-way retrieval, improving the consistency between atmospheric state space and radiance observation space, which could enhance NWP accuracy. The framework jointly performs atmospheric profile retrieval and radiance reconstruction, using a bidirectional Mamba module to capture long-range dependencies along pressure levels, and outperforms several deep learning baselines on temperature and humidity retrieval as well as radiance reconstruction tasks.

## [EEG Foundation Models Boost Burst-Suppression Detection in ICU](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20074) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper is the first to evaluate EEG foundation models (REVE-base, LUNA-large, LuMamba-Tiny) for automatic burst-suppression detection in ICU EEG without patient-specific calibration, introducing an event-based evaluation method. REVE-base achieved the highest event-based F1-score of 0.868 and reduced burst-per-minute error by 52.1% compared to EEGNet. Burst-suppression detection is critical for monitoring sedation depth in ICU patients, but manual annotation is scarce and patterns vary across patients. This work demonstrates that EEG foundation models can enable scalable, calibration-free monitoring, potentially improving clinical decision-making in critical care. The study compared three EEG foundation models (REVE-base, LUNA-large, LuMamba-Tiny) against an adaptive thresholding baseline and a task-specific EEGNet baseline. Full fine-tuning was the most effective adaptation strategy, and with only 25% of labeled data, REVE-base outperformed random initialization by +0.723 event-based F1 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Burst suppression is an EEG pattern characterized by alternating high-amplitude bursts and low-amplitude suppression, used to assess sedation depth in ICU patients under induced coma. EEG foundation models are large-scale models pre-trained on diverse EEG data via self-supervised learning, which can be adapted to downstream tasks with limited labeled data. Traditional window-based classification may miss clinically relevant burst episodes, motivating the event-based evaluation approach.

## [MedRLM: Recursive Multimodal Framework for Clinical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20164) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MedRLM is a recursive multimodal framework that decomposes patient cases into inspectable components for long-context clinical reasoning, sensor-guided screening, and community-to-tertiary referral optimization. This framework addresses limitations of current LLM-based systems by enabling auditable, multimodal, and workflow-aware clinical decision support, potentially improving healthcare AI's reliability and integration into real-world workflows. MedRLM introduces a Clinical Evidence Graph Memory to connect patient-specific observations with retrieved evidence, and uses a sensor-guided recursive triggering mechanism for deeper reasoning when abnormal patterns are detected.

**Background**: Current medical AI systems often rely on single-step prompting or retrieval, which can be fragile when clinical evidence is distributed across long electronic health records, medical images, and sensor streams. MedRLM treats the patient case as an external environment that can be recursively inspected, decomposed, and synthesized, moving beyond static question answering.

## [RS-Neg: First Benchmark for Negation in Remote Sensing MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20177) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces RS-Neg, the first benchmark specifically designed to evaluate negation comprehension in remote sensing multimodal large language models (MLLMs), and proposes NeFo, a test-time learning method that improves negation understanding using about 5% unlabeled test samples. Negation comprehension is critical for real-world remote sensing applications, such as identifying non-flooded evacuation routes, and this work addresses a significant gap in current MLLMs, potentially improving their reliability and safety in high-stakes scenarios. RS-Neg covers region-level to scene-level tasks and uses an automated data generation pipeline with LLMs to synthesize diverse negation queries, while NeFo explicitly incorporates the logical role of negation into model optimization at test time, showing strong generalization to unseen tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) combine vision and language to perform tasks like visual question answering. However, they often struggle with negation (e.g., recognizing what is not present), which is essential for precise reasoning in applications like emergency response. Test-time learning adapts models during inference using unlabeled data, offering a lightweight way to improve performance without retraining.

## [ELVA: Rule-Based RL to Overcome Grain Blindness in Multimodal Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20280) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ELVA introduces a rule-based reinforcement learning framework that treats negative samples differently based on their similarity to positives, addressing grain blindness in universal multimodal retrieval. It also presents MRBench, a new benchmark for multi-grain query scenarios, and achieves state-of-the-art results with a 13.1% improvement on MRBench. Grain blindness has been a overlooked issue in multimodal retrieval, and ELVA's ranking-driven approach provides a principled solution that can improve retrieval accuracy for complex queries. This work could influence future contrastive learning methods in multimodal AI systems. ELVA extends Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) to retrieval tasks, using rule-based rewards to jointly optimize negative sample ranking and enlarge the positive-negative similarity gap. The framework does not rely on reward models or explicit ranking labels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Universal Multimodal Retrieval (UMR) aims to search across modalities like text, image, video, and audio using a unified model. Traditional contrastive learning treats samples as binary positive/negative, ignoring the varying information in negatives, which leads to grain blindness—the model's inability to capture fine-grained query details. ELVA addresses this by ranking negatives based on their similarity to positives.

## [BES: Adaptive Contrastive Learning for Graph Disentanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20283) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Boundary Embedding Shaping (BES), an adaptive contrastive learning module for GNNs that selectively suppresses spurious structural noise at decision boundaries to improve graph structural disentanglement. This work addresses a key limitation in GNNs—graph structural entanglement—by focusing on boundary nodes, which are most vulnerable to noise. BES consistently improves node classification and link prediction, outperforming existing robust GNN methods. BES is a plug-in module that requires minimal parameter perturbation. Experiments show BES boosts GCN performance by an average of 3.3% in node classification (up to 5.0% on WikiCS) and achieves superior accuracy in link prediction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Graph neural networks (GNNs) aggregate neighbor information for node classification, but spurious correlations from semantically irrelevant neighbors can contaminate embeddings—a problem known as graph structural entanglement. This issue is most severe for nodes near class boundaries, where noise blurs decision boundaries. Existing robust GNN methods treat all nodes uniformly, ignoring boundary vulnerabilities.

Researchers propose using multiple decompiler views (Ghidra and RetDec) to improve LLM-based malware classification, showing that combining both views increases recall on malicious samples. This simple, training-free approach addresses the fragility of relying on a single decompiler, potentially improving malware triage in security operations and making LLM-based analysis more robust. The study curates a benchmark of benign and malicious binaries, decompiles each with both Ghidra and RetDec, and tests across multiple LLM families, finding that multi-view prompting improves malicious-class F1 mainly by boosting recall.

## [Repurposing Speech Classifier for Diffusion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20457) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose using a frozen speech classifier as the backbone for guided diffusion-based speech generation, attaching a lightweight subnetwork trained with Denoising Score Matching to reuse intermediate classifier representations. This approach eliminates the need for a separate diffusion model, reducing memory footprint and computational cost while maintaining high speech quality, potentially making conditional speech synthesis more efficient and accessible. The method operates in log-Mel spectrogram space and uses a noise-conditioned classifier trained conventionally, with only the lightweight subnetwork trained under the Denoising Score Matching objective.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Classifier guidance in diffusion models typically requires two separately trained models: a classifier and a diffusion model. This work explores a more compact alternative by repurposing a single classifier for both classification and generation, bridging discriminative and generative modeling.

## [FreeStyle: Scalable Dual-Reference Image Generation via LoRA Mining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20506) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FreeStyle proposes a scalable framework for style-content dual-reference image generation that mines community LoRAs to construct large-scale triplets and uses a two-stage curriculum with attention-level and frequency-aware mechanisms to prevent content leakage. This work addresses a key bottleneck in dual-reference generation—the lack of large-scale, cleanly separated style-content triplet data—by leveraging community LoRAs, potentially enabling more robust and diverse stylized image generation for the generative AI community. FreeStyle introduces a rigorous generation and filtering pipeline to construct triplets across multiple base models, and employs a two-stage curriculum: an attention-level enrichment constraint for style-transfer stage and a frequency-aware RoPE modulation for dual-reference stage to combat leakage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Style-content dual-reference generation aims to create an image that keeps the content of one reference while adopting the style of another. A major challenge is content leakage, where unwanted content from the style reference appears in the output. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a technique for fine-tuning large models efficiently, and community LoRAs are user-shared adaptations that cover diverse styles. FreeStyle mines these LoRAs to generate training triplets with clean style-content separation.

Researchers propose AAPA (Adversarially Anchored Preference Alignment), a plug-in framework that adds a sentence-level adversarial anchoring signal to existing post-training methods like SFT, GRPO, and CHORD, without requiring online teacher inference or co-training. AAPA addresses overfitting and reward exploitation in LLM post-training, consistently improving instruction-following benchmarks across model scales (e.g., +5.77% on Qwen3-0.6B, +3.75% on Qwen3-4B over GRPO), offering a lightweight and compatible enhancement for alignment pipelines. AAPA uses a fixed lightweight discriminator to compare policy rollouts with offline expert responses, providing a stable semantic grounding signal. It is plug-in compatible with SFT, GRPO, and CHORD, preserving their original pipelines.

## [ML Models Compared for Power System Fault Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00831) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a controlled comparison of machine learning models for fault classification and localization in power systems, using a common electromagnetic transient dataset and decision windows of 10-50 ms. The best models achieve F1 scores above 0.98 for fault classification at 10 ms and localization error of about 10% of normalized line length. This study addresses reproducibility issues in power system ML research by providing a standardized benchmark, enabling fair comparison of models. The findings help researchers and engineers select appropriate models for protection tasks, especially with the increasing complexity from inverter-based resources. The study uses decision windows of 10-50 ms to reflect protection-relevant time scales, and finds that lower-capacity models degrade at shorter horizons but improve with longer windows. Line-resolved analysis shows localization accuracy varies across grid segments, indicating topology-dependent difficulty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Modern power systems increasingly integrate inverter-based and distributed energy resources, which challenge conventional protection schemes. Machine learning offers potential solutions, but published results are often incomparable due to varying datasets, sensing assumptions, and decision horizons. This paper provides a controlled comparison using a common electromagnetic transient dataset and standardized conditions.

## [PrototypeNAS: Zero-Shot NAS for MCU DNN Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15106) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PrototypeNAS introduces a zero-shot neural architecture search method that decouples DNN design from training, enabling rapid specialization of deep neural networks for microcontroller units within minutes. This approach significantly reduces the computational cost and time of NAS, making it practical for resource-constrained edge devices. It enables efficient deployment of DNNs on MCUs, which are ubiquitous in IoT and embedded systems. PrototypeNAS uses a three-step search: a novel search space combining multiple architecture types with pruning and quantization, an ensemble of zero-shot proxies, and Hypervolume subset selection to distill Pareto-optimal architectures. It was evaluated on 12 datasets across image classification, time series classification, and object detection.

## [MoCA-Agent: Market-of-Claims for Financial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11537) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MoCA-Agent introduces a market-of-claims mechanism where specialist trader agents buy or sell atomic claims, and a code-aware verifier checks the generated Python program for errors, achieving strong results on ten financial and numerical reasoning benchmarks. This approach improves robustness in high-stakes numerical reasoning by aggregating evidence at the claim level rather than whole answers, reducing silent errors in financial reporting, audit, and decision-support workflows. Using a fixed Qwen3.6-27B backbone, MoCA-Agent achieves 78.3% on FinQA, 76.0% on FinanceMath, 71.2% on MultiHiertt, 86.9% on ESGenius, and 85.6% average on FinChart-Bench, with at most one market-aware repair round.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Financial and tabular question answering requires exact grounding in facts, formulas, and units. Traditional multi-agent debate systems exchange free-form arguments, which can produce plausible but wrong answers. MoCA-Agent replaces this with a structured market-of-claims mechanism that decomposes questions into typed atomic claims and uses specialist traders to verify each claim independently.

## [New Task and Dataset for Drug-Disease Applicability Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14031) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a new task, applicability condition extraction for therapeutic drug-disease relations, and presents the first manually annotated dataset of 1,119 drug-disease pairs from biomedical abstracts. The authors also propose a method that enhances LoRA to incorporate drug-disease relations, outperforming existing baselines. Existing biomedical information extraction methods focus on identifying drug-disease relations but ignore the context-specific conditions under which drugs are effective. This work fills that gap, enabling more precise clinical decision support and advancing biomedical NLP. The dataset contains 1,119 drug-disease pairs with manually annotated applicability conditions extracted from paper abstracts. The proposed method modifies LoRA to explicitly model relations between drugs and diseases, achieving consistent improvements over strong baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained model weights and injects trainable low-rank matrices, reducing trainable parameters for downstream tasks. Biomedical information extraction typically identifies relations between entities (e.g., drug-disease) but often misses the nuanced conditions that qualify those relations, such as dosage or patient population.

## [Scaffolding Boosts Human-AI Team Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18413) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study on human-AI collaboration in shared workspaces shows that adding structured scaffolding—combining shared memory with human-in-the-loop (HITL) approval gates—significantly improves team performance, especially in three-person teams, based on 1,482 sessions in the Collaborative Gym environment. This research provides empirical evidence that how human-AI teams coordinate matters as much as the AI's capability, offering practical design principles for building more effective collaborative AI systems in scientific and professional tasks. The study used the Collaborative Gym framework with DiscoveryBench tasks, finding that adding collaborators without structure can lower performance due to coordination overhead, while scaffolding with shared memory and HITL gates yields clearer responsibility signals and better expertise routing.

The Collaborative Gym (Co-Gym) is a multi-agent framework for enabling and evaluating such collaboration. Human-in-the-loop (HITL) gates pause workflows at specific steps for human approval, ensuring human oversight in automated processes.

A new study systematically evaluates Sionna v1.0.2 ray-tracing for outdoor cellular links in central Rome, finding that antenna locations and orientations critically affect simulation fidelity, while solver hyper-parameters have negligible impact. This work provides empirical evidence that ray-tracing simulations for machine learning-based RF tasks must prioritize accurate antenna modeling and geometry over solver tuning, highlighting a key challenge for transferable high-fidelity outdoor RF simulation. Using 1,664 user equipments and six base stations, the study measured Spearman correlation and k-nearest-neighbor localization error; greedy optimization improved correlation by 5% to 130% and reduced localization error by one-third, though error remained twice that of real data.

This work addresses a critical gap in LLM security by providing a fingerprinting method that resists both defensive filtering and model modifications, enabling reliable black-box ownership verification to prevent unauthorized redistribution. CF uses code-mixing across five languages to create natural-looking yet robust triggers, while MCEdit constructs redundant trigger-target mappings that degrade gracefully under model modification. Extensive evaluations show negligible impact on utility.

MENTOR uses a process-aware reward that balances behavioral alignment with downstream performance, avoiding the sparse reward and strict trajectory matching problems of standard RL. Experiments on executable-tool benchmarks show improved OOD performance over SFT and strict RL baselines.

Researchers propose Visual Attentive Prompting (VAP), a training-free perceptual adapter that enables frozen Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models to follow personalized commands like 'bring my cup' using only a few reference images. This work addresses a critical gap in VLA models—personalized object manipulation—without requiring retraining, making it practical for real-world robotics applications where users need robots to recognize and act on their specific belongings. ## [BA-Solver Accelerates Flow Matching Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21542) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose the Bi-Anchor Interpolation Solver (BA-solver), a training-free ODE solver for flow matching generative models that introduces a lightweight SideNet (1-2% backbone size) to achieve bidirectional temporal perception and bi-anchor velocity integration, enabling high-quality generation in as few as 5-10 neural function evaluations. BA-solver bridges the gap between training-free solvers (which degrade at low NFEs) and training-based methods (which are costly and not plug-and-play), offering a practical acceleration solution for flow matching models that can be seamlessly integrated into existing pipelines, potentially reducing inference latency for generative AI applications. On ImageNet-256², BA-solver achieves generation quality comparable to a 100+ NFE Euler solver in just 10 NFEs, and maintains high fidelity in as few as 5 NFEs. The SideNet is trained with negligible cost while the backbone remains frozen, and the method supports downstream tasks like image editing.

**Background**: Flow matching models generate data by learning a velocity field that transforms a simple distribution into a target distribution via an ordinary differential equation (ODE). Solving this ODE typically requires many neural function evaluations (NFEs), creating a latency bottleneck. Existing acceleration approaches either sacrifice quality (training-free solvers) or require expensive retraining (distillation or one-step methods).

## [DeFrame: Debiasing LLMs Against Framing Effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04306) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce 'framing disparity' as a hidden bias in LLMs, showing that fairness evaluations vary with prompt phrasing, and propose a framing-aware debiasing method to reduce these disparities. This work reveals a new source of bias in LLMs that standard fairness evaluations miss, and offers a practical debiasing technique, advancing AI safety and fairness research. The method augments fairness benchmarks with alternative framings (e.g., 'A is better than B' vs. 'B is worse than A'), measures framing disparity, and applies a framing-aware training objective to improve consistency across framings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in real-world applications, making fairness crucial. However, hidden biases can persist even when models pass standard fairness tests. Framing effects, where semantically equivalent prompts phrased differently lead to different outputs, are a known cognitive bias in humans but underexplored in LLMs.

## [Flickering Multi-Armed Bandits: A New Model with Regret Bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17315) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Flickering Multi-Armed Bandits (FMAB), a novel bandit variant where action availability depends on the previous action, modeled via stochastic graphs. They provide sublinear regret bounds and a matching lower bound, validated with a disaster-response simulation. This work bridges the gap between traditional bandits and real-world scenarios like robotics and disaster response, where movement constraints are critical. The theoretical guarantees enable principled algorithm design for such environments. The FMAB model uses i.i.d. Erdős–Rényi and Edge-Markovian graphs to represent changing action neighborhoods. The proposed two-phase lazy random walk algorithm achieves high-probability sublinear regret, and the lower bound shows near-optimality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-armed bandits are a framework for sequential decision-making under uncertainty, where an agent chooses actions to maximize cumulative reward. Traditional models assume all actions are always available, but in many applications, such as navigation, the set of available actions depends on the current state. The FMAB model captures this by restricting actions to a local neighborhood in a graph, which evolves stochastically.

ZeSTA introduces a domain-conditioned training framework that uses lightweight domain embeddings to distinguish real from synthetic speech, improving speaker similarity in low-resource personalized speech synthesis when augmented with zero-shot TTS. This work addresses a critical issue in low-resource TTS: naive synthetic augmentation degrades speaker similarity. ZeSTA's simple yet effective approach can enable high-quality personalized speech synthesis with minimal real data, benefiting applications like voice cloning and assistive technology. ZeSTA does not modify the base TTS architecture; it only adds a small domain embedding and uses real-data oversampling. Experiments on LibriTTS and an in-house dataset with two ZS-TTS sources show improved speaker similarity while maintaining intelligibility and perceptual quality.

KG-SoftMAP integrates soft knowledge graph priors into Bayesian network structure learning, achieving Directed-F1 scores of 0.19–0.32 at observation rate 0.05 and 0.44–0.97 at rate ≥0.2 on synthetic benchmarks, while data-only methods stay near zero. This work addresses the hard problem of learning Bayesian networks from extremely sparse discrete data, where traditional data-only methods fail. By leveraging imperfect domain knowledge from knowledge graphs or LLMs, it enables structure recovery in real-world scenarios like education with limited observations. The method uses a logit-form prior derived from a weighted directed knowledge graph and maximizes a MAP objective combining the BDeu score with the prior. On real educational datasets, the learned BN matches logistic regression within 0.03 F1_FAIL while providing an interpretable concept graph and calibrated probabilities.

TOTEN is a knowledge-based ontological tokenization framework that replaces statistical Byte-Pair Encoding with declarative classification grounded in a formal ontology of engineering entities (OEE), specifically designed for physical quantities and technical notation in Brazilian Portuguese. This work addresses a critical limitation of standard tokenizers in engineering and scientific NLP, where fragmentation of units, numbers, and symbols degrades downstream tasks. TOTEN achieves significantly higher numerical reconstruction accuracy (0.780 vs. 0.340 on EngQuant benchmark) compared to state-of-the-art baselines, promising better performance in technical document processing. TOTEN is formalized as a triple (ontology, classification function, instantiator family) and integrates three external oracles: Pint for dimensional analysis, Unicode Character Database for typographic robustness, and RSLP for Portuguese morphology. It was evaluated on an internal benchmark (EngQuant, N=800) and four external Brazilian Portuguese corpora (N=1771 eligible cases), demonstrating statistically significant improvements over eight baselines.

**Background**: Byte-Pair Encoding (BPE) is a widely used tokenization algorithm in large language models that compresses vocabulary by merging frequent character pairs, but it is semantically blind to structured entities like physical quantities. Ontological tokenization uses a formal ontology to define types and relations, enabling deterministic classification of technical terms. TOTEN's ontology of engineering entities (OEE) captures types, structural principles, composition relations, and preservable invariants for physical quantities and technical notation.

## [ProMUSE: Adaptive Multimodal Alzheimer's Diagnosis with Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19371) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ProMUSE is a progressive multimodal network that uses evidential deep learning to quantify uncertainty from low-cost clinical data and decides whether to acquire expensive MRI or PET scans only when uncertainty is high, reducing imaging usage by 50-90% while maintaining accuracy. This approach addresses the practical challenge of high cost and limited accessibility of multimodal imaging in Alzheimer's diagnosis, making early screening more feasible in resource-constrained clinical settings and potentially reducing healthcare costs. ProMUSE uses a Dirichlet-based subjective logic model for uncertainty quantification and Dempster-Shafer theory for fusing modality-wise belief and uncertainty. It was evaluated on ADNI, AIBL, and OASIS datasets across three classification tasks (CN-AD, CN-MCI, MCI-AD), achieving competitive accuracy with full-modality baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 20, 04:00

**Background**: Alzheimer's disease diagnosis increasingly relies on multimodal data like clinical assessments, MRI, and PET scans, but MRI and PET are costly and not universally accessible. Evidential deep learning quantifies both aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty in a single forward pass, enabling models to know when they are uncertain. ProMUSE leverages this to decide when additional modalities are needed, reducing unnecessary imaging.
